{
  "abstract_verbatim": "Let <i>Y</i> be smooth projective and let <i>C</i> be an effective integral divisor. If $`-K_Y+C`$ admits a metric with a global strict curvature lower bound for which the canonical section of <i>C</i> is locally square integrable, every pseudoeffective rational divisor becomes rationally effective after an effective correction supported on <i>C</i>. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>Y</i> be smooth projective and let <i>C</i> be an effective integral divisor. If $`-K_Y+C`$ admits a metric with a global strict curvature lower bound for which the canonical section of <i>C</i> is locally square integrable, every pseudoeffective rational divisor becomes rationally effective after an effective correction supported on <i>C</i>. For a smooth projective <i>X</i> with smoothly semipositive $`L=-K_X`$, sections of $`m_jL-P`$ at unbounded positive exponents, with any fixed pseudoeffective Cartier error <i>P</i>, yield a section of a positive multiple of <i>L</i>.

- **claim-controlled-boundary-effectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-controlled-boundary)): Let $Y$ be a smooth projective complex variety of positive dimension and let $C\ge0$ be an integral divisor. Suppose $-K_Y+C$ has a singular Hermitian metric $h$ whose curvature dominates a Kähler form and for which $$\mathcal J(h)\supset\mathcal O_Y(-C).$$ For every pseudoeffective rational divisor $M$ on $Y$, there is an effective rational divisor $C_0$ supported on $C$ such that $M+C_0$ is rationally effective.
  - Explanation: Strict anticanonical curvature and integrability of the distinguished boundary section make each pseudoeffective rational divisor rationally effective after a correction confined to that boundary.
- **claim-weighted-resolution-discrepancies** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-resolution)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective, let $J\ge0$ be an integral divisor, and let $B$ be a rational line bundle with a singular metric $h$ having a global strict curvature lower bound. Suppose $\mathcal J(h)\supset\mathcal O_Y(-J)$. For every sufficiently large divisible integer $r$, the complete system $\|rB\|$ is nonempty. Let $\sigma:Y'\to Y$ be a log resolution of its base ideal, $J$, and the exceptional locus, and write $$\sigma^*\|rB\|=G+\|\mathrm{Mov}\|,$$ where $G$ is the fixed divisor and $\|\mathrm{Mov}\|$ is base-point free. Then the simple-normal-crossing divisor $$\label{eq:weighted-signed-divisor} \Psi=G/r-\sigma^*J-K_{Y'/Y}$$ has every coefficient strictly less than one. Its negative part is supported on $\sigma^*J$ and the exceptional primes.
  - Explanation: Approximation preserves the distinguished section's integrability as strict discrepancy inequalities. Negative contributions occur only on the pulled-back boundary and exceptional primes.
- **claim-weighted-adjoint-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-weighted-approximation)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective and let $J\ge0$ be integral. Suppose $-K_Y+J$ has a singular metric with a global strict curvature lower bound and multiplier ideal containing $\mathcal O_Y(-J)$. Choose a sufficiently small ample rational divisor $A$. For a sufficiently large divisible integer $r$, resolve the complete system $\|r(-K_Y+J-A)\|$ and $J$ by $\sigma:Y'\to Y$, and write $$\sigma^*\|r(-K_Y+J-A)\|=G+\|\mathrm{Mov}\|.$$ Then $\Psi=G/r-\sigma^*J-K_{Y'/Y}$ has coefficients less than one, $\Psi^+$ is an effective klt boundary, and $D_0=\Psi^-$ is effective and supported on $\sigma^*J$ and exceptional primes. Moreover $$\label{eq:weighted-adjoint-identity} D_0\sim_{\mathbb Q} K_{Y'}+\Psi^++\mathrm{Mov}/r+\sigma^*A.$$ The rational class $\mathrm{Mov}/r+\sigma^*A$ is nef and big. If an algebraic torus acts on $Y$, $J$ is invariant, and the indicated full systems are torus-stable, the resolution may be chosen equivariantly. Then $G$, $K_{Y'/Y}$, $\Psi^\pm$ and $D_0$ are invariant, and the free system $\|\mathrm{Mov}\|$ is torus-stable. Identity <a href="#eq:weighted-adjoint-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weighted-adjoint-identity">[eq:weighted-adjoint-identity]</a> is an identity of ordinary rational line bundles; it does not assert that a chosen member of either the free system or the ample class is invariant.
  - Explanation: The signed approximation separates an effective klt boundary from a supported correction and retains a nef and big moving class. Equivariance controls the systems and divisors, while the adjoint identity remains an ordinary line-bundle identity.
- **claim-rational-linear-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-recovery)): Let $D$ be a rational divisor on a normal projective variety. If $D$ is real-linearly equivalent to an effective real divisor, then it is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor.
  - Explanation: For a rational divisor, existence of an effective real-linear representative implies existence of an effective rational-linear representative.
- **claim-fixed-correction-before-scaling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-ample-perturbation)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:controlled-boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:controlled-boundary">1</a>, there is an effective rational divisor $F$ supported on $C$ such that for every pseudoeffective rational divisor $M$, some positive rational $t$ makes $F+tM$ rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor.
  - Explanation: The supported correction can be fixed before selecting the pseudoeffective divisor. The positive scaling parameter may depend on that divisor.
- **claim-integrable-pseudoeffective-perturbation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-pseudoeffective-perturbation)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:controlled-boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:controlled-boundary">1</a>, for every pseudoeffective rational divisor $M$ there is a positive rational $\epsilon$ such that $-K_Y+C+\epsilon M$ has a metric with a global strict curvature lower bound and multiplier ideal containing $\mathcal O_Y(-C)$.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently small pseudoeffective metric perturbation preserves both the global strict curvature bound and integrability of the canonical boundary section.
- **claim-simultaneous-integrable-neighborhood** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-integrable-neighborhood)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective of positive dimension and let $B$ be a rational line bundle. Suppose $B$ has a semipositive singular metric with locally integrable squared frame norms, smooth and strictly positive on a nonempty ordinary open set. For any finite list of rational line bundles $P_1,\ldots,P_s$, there are $\epsilon\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$ and a common ample rational line bundle $H'$ such that each $B+\epsilon P_i-H'$ is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective divisor $\Delta_i$ with $(Y,\Delta_i)$ klt.
  - Explanation: Unweighted frame integrability and positivity on an ordinary open set permit simultaneous small perturbations in finitely many rational directions, with a common ample reserve and effective klt representatives.
- **claim-big-klt-representatives** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-big-klt-representative)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:integrable-neighborhood" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:integrable-neighborhood">7</a>, $B$ has an effective big klt rational representative. The same holds for $B+\epsilon P_i$ for the sufficiently small simultaneous perturbations in that lemma.
  - Explanation: Adding a sufficiently general ample representative yields effective big klt representatives for the original line bundle and its simultaneous small perturbations.
- **claim-correction-ring-finite-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-correction-ring)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective of positive dimension, and let $A_0$ be a rational divisor. Suppose $-K_Y+A_0$ has a semipositive singular metric with locally integrable squared frame norms, smooth and strictly positive on a nonempty ordinary open set. Given rational divisors $P_1,\ldots,P_s$, there are $\epsilon\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$ and an integer $k_0>0$ such that the actual divisors $B_i=k_0(A_0+\epsilon P_i)$ are Cartier and $$R(Y;B_1,\ldots,B_s)= \bigoplus_{(a_1,\ldots,a_s)\in\mathbb N^s} H^0\!\left(Y,\mathcal O_Y\Bigl(\sum_i a_iB_i\Bigr)\right)$$ is a finitely generated $\mathbb C$-algebra.
  - Explanation: Nearby correction divisors admit common Cartier multiples whose actual multigraded section ring is finitely generated. The conclusion concerns rational linear equivalence and specified divisors.
- **claim-fixed-pseudoeffective-error-conversion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conv-fixed-error)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety and let $L=-K_X$ have a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Let $P$ be a pseudoeffective Cartier divisor. If there are effective integral $N_j\sim m_jL-P$ for unbounded positive integers $m_j$, then $H^0(X,mL)\ne0$ for some positive integer $m$.
  - Explanation: An unbounded positive sequence of effective anticanonical twists with a fixed pseudoeffective Cartier error supplies a section of an untwisted positive anticanonical multiple.
- **claim-exceptional-correction-return** (construction, [source](#stmt-conv-return)): Let $\pi:Z\to X$ be a projective birational morphism of smooth projective varieties and let $f:Z\to Y$ be a surjective morphism to a smooth projective variety with connected general fibers, and let $L$ be a rational line bundle on $X$. Assume every prime of $Z$ mapping into codimension at least two in $Y$ is $\pi$-exceptional. Let $R\sim_{\mathbb Q}a\pi^*L$, $a>0$, be a rational divisor with nonnegative horizontal part. For each base prime $Q$ put $$\mu_Q(R)=\min_{D\mapsto Q} \frac{\operatorname{ord}_D R}{\operatorname{ord}_D f^*Q}, \qquad M=\sum_Q\mu_Q(R)Q.$$ Suppose $M$ is pseudoeffective. If there is an effective rational $C_0$, all components of whose pullback are $\pi$-exceptional, such that $M+C_0\sim_{\mathbb Q}G\ge0$, then $L$ is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor. The same conclusion holds if the premise is $\epsilon M+C_0\sim_{\mathbb Q}G\ge0$ for some rational $\epsilon>0$.* *For the invariant conclusion, give $L$ a specified linearization and assume $$R=c\,\operatorname{div}(\pi^*t),\qquad cr=a,$$ where $c>0$ is rational and $t$ is a nonzero invariant rational section of the specified linearized $L^r$, with $r>0$ an integer for which $L^r$ is a line bundle. Assume also that every function pulled back from $Y$ is invariant in $\mathbb C(X)$. Then the resulting section of a positive multiple of $L$ is invariant for that linearization; no regular group action on $Z$ is required.
  - Explanation: Vertical minima and exceptionality of the correction ensure that a principal adjustment from the base pushes forward effectively. Invariance additionally requires an invariant rational section and invariant pulled-back base functions.
- **claim-null-curvature-kernel-inclusion** (bound, [source](#stmt-conv-null-curvature)): Let $Z$ be smooth projective of dimension $n$, and let $\theta,\eta$ be smooth semipositive closed $(1,1)$-forms. Suppose $a[\theta]-[\eta]$ is pseudoeffective for some $a>0$. If $\nu$ is the maximum rank of $\theta$, then $\ker\theta\subset\ker\eta$ on its rank-$\nu$ locus. If $\eta=f^*\omega_Y$ on an ordinary open meeting the rank-$\nu$ locus, with $f$ submersive and $\omega_Y>0$, there is a smaller nonempty patch on which $\theta\ge c f^*\omega_Y$ for some $c>0$.
  - Explanation: A pseudoeffective comparison between smooth semipositive forms forces kernel inclusion on the maximum-rank locus and gives horizontal strictness on an appropriate smaller patch.
- **claim-unweighted-correction-effectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conv-unweighted-effectivity)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective of positive dimension and $A_0\ge0$ rational. Suppose $B=-K_Y+A_0$ has a semipositive metric with locally integrable squared frame norms, smooth and strictly positive on a nonempty ordinary open. For every pseudoeffective rational $M$ there is a rational $\epsilon>0$ such that $A_0+\epsilon M$ is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor.
  - Explanation: An effective rational correction carrying an unweighted integrable semipositive adjoint metric admits rational effectivity after a sufficiently small pseudoeffective perturbation.
- **claim-determinant-fixed-error-extraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-conv-determinant)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective, let $L=-K_X$ be nef, and fix $p>0$. If $H^0(X,(\Omega_X^1)^{\otimes p}\otimes mL)\ne0$ for unbounded positive integers $m$, there are a pseudoeffective Cartier divisor $P$ and effective integral $N_j\sim m_jL-P$ for unbounded positive $m_j$. If the starting sections are invariant for a torus and the induced linearizations, the determinant sections defining $N_j$ are invariant.
  - Explanation: Saturating the generic span of twisted cotangent sections and taking its determinant produces a fixed pseudoeffective error and effective divisors at unbounded positive exponents. Equivariant determinant extraction preserves invariant input sections.
- **claim-ordinary-tensor-conversion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conv-tensor-conversion)): Under the metric hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#conv:fixed-error" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conv:fixed-error">10</a>, if $H^0(X,(\Omega_X^1)^{\otimes p}\otimes mL)\ne0$ for a fixed $p\ge0$ and unbounded positive $m$, then a positive multiple of $L$ has a section. The same holds with $\Omega_X^p$ in place of the tensor power.
  - Explanation: A fixed cotangent tensor degree with sections at unbounded positive twists gives an ordinary anticanonical section under smooth metric semipositivity. Exterior forms satisfy the same conclusion.
- **claim-primitive-direct-image-curvature** (bound, [source](#stmt-coa-curvature)): Let $f:\mathcal Z\to\mathbb D$ be a proper holomorphic submersion from a Kähler manifold, with fibers of complex dimension $r>0$. Let $(\mathcal L,h)$ be a holomorphic line bundle with a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature. Suppose $\mathcal E=f_*(K_{\mathcal Z/\mathbb D}+\mathcal L)$ is locally free and commutes with base change. Give $\mathcal E$ its fiberwise $L^2$ metric. Write $D'$ for the $(1,0)$ part of the Chern connection of this direct-image metric. For a local holomorphic section $v$ with $D'v(0)=0$, there is a smooth absolute $\mathcal L$-valued $(r,0)$-form $w$ representing $v$ such that $$\label{coa:curvature-bound} -i\partial\bar\partial\\|v\\|^2\big\|_0 \ \geq\ f_*\bigl(c_r w\wedge\overline w\,h\wedge i\Theta_{\mathcal L,h}\bigr) \big\|_0,\qquad c_r=i^{r^2}.$$ In particular, if $i\Theta_{\mathcal L,h}\geq c f^*(i\,dt\wedge d\bar t)$ on a nonempty open subset of the central fiber and its neighborhood, where $c>0$ and $v(0)$ does not vanish identically on that subset, then the curvature pairing of $\mathcal E$ with $v(0)$ is strictly positive at $0$.
  - Explanation: A Chern-normal section admits an absolute representative yielding a lower bound for direct-image curvature. A horizontal strictness patch where the section is nonzero makes the curvature pairing strictly positive.
- **claim-coarea-higher-integrability** (bound, [source](#stmt-coa-coarea)): Let $f:Z\to Y$ be a surjective morphism between connected smooth projective varieties, with $\dim Z=n$ and $\dim Y=b>0$. Let $\mu$ be a smooth nonnegative top-degree form on $Z$. Then $f_*\mu$ is absolutely continuous with respect to any smooth positive volume on $Y$, and its density belongs to $L^{1+\eta}(Y)$ for some $\eta>0$.
  - Explanation: The pushforward of a smooth nonnegative volume form has no singular measure on the exceptional base set and has a density with a strictly positive integrability margin.
- **claim-supported-smooth-volume-metric** (construction, [source](#stmt-coa-volume-metric)): Let $X$ and $Z$ be connected smooth projective varieties, let $g:Z\to X$ be a birational morphism, and let $f:Z\to Y$ be a surjective morphism to a connected smooth projective variety of positive dimension. Put $L=-K_X$, and assume $L$ has a smooth Hermitian metric $h$ with semipositive curvature. Let $E=K_{Z/X}$ be the effective Jacobian divisor. Assume $$\operatorname{rank} f_*\mathcal O_Z(E)=1.$$ Let $\mathcal S$ be the set of prime divisors $G$ on $Y$ such that every prime divisor of $Z$ dominating $G$ is $g$-exceptional. Suppose there is an open patch over the smooth base-change locus of $f$, disjoint from $E$, on which the curvature of $g^*h$ dominates a positive multiple of the pullback of a Kähler form on $Y$. Then there is an effective integral divisor $A$ supported on $\mathcal S$ and a singular metric on $M=-K_Y+A$, with psh weights $\psi$, such that the metric is smooth and strictly positively curved on a nonempty open subset of $Y$ and $$\label{coa:weighted-lp} e^{-\psi}\|s_A\|^2\in L^{1+\eta}_{\mathrm{loc}} \qquad\text{for some }\eta>0.$$ Here $\|s_A\|$ means the absolute value of the coefficient of the canonical section in a local holomorphic frame.
  - Explanation: Ordinary adjoint rank and horizontal strictness give an integral correction supported on exceptional base primes and an extended semipositive metric whose distinguished section has higher integrability.
- **claim-invariant-volume-metric** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-coa-invariant-volume-metric)): The conclusion of Proposition <a href="#coa:volume-metric" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="coa:volume-metric">18</a> remains valid if its ordinary rank-one hypothesis is replaced by $$\operatorname{rank}\bigl(f_*\mathcal O_Z(K_{Z/X})\bigr)^T=1,$$ provided that a compact torus $T$ acts holomorphically on $X$ and $Z$, acts trivially on $Y$, preserves $f$, $g$, and the smooth metric $h$, and all canonical bundles carry their natural linearizations.
  - Explanation: The smooth volume-metric construction also works with invariant adjoint rank, provided compact averaging is an orthogonal holomorphic projection and the action preserves the geometric and metric data.
- **claim-weighted-local-to-global-effectivity** (construction, [source](#stmt-coa-weighted-effectivity)): Let $Y$ be smooth connected projective of positive dimension, let $A\geq0$ be integral, and put $M=-K_Y+A$. Suppose $M$ has psh metric weights $\psi$, smooth and strictly positively curved on a nonempty open set, and $$\|s_A\|^2e^{-\psi}\in L^{1+\eta}_{\mathrm{loc}} \quad\text{for some }\eta>0.$$ Then $M$ has a singular metric with a global strict curvature lower bound and with $\|s_A\|^2$ times its squared frame norm locally integrable. For every pseudoeffective rational divisor $W$, an effective rational divisor $C_A$ supported on $A$ makes $W+C_A$ rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor.
  - Explanation: Higher integrability permits mixing the locally strict metric with a globally strict metric while retaining integrability of the distinguished section. Controlled-boundary effectivity then supplies a supported correction.
- **claim-jet-supported-effectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-jet-base-lemma)): Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective variety of dimension $b>0$, let $A\geq0$ be an integral divisor, and put $M=-K_Y+A$. Assume $M$ has a singular Hermitian metric with psh local weights $\varphi$, smooth and strictly positively curved on a nonempty open subset, and satisfying $$\label{jet:weighted-assumption} e^{-\varphi}\|s_A\|^2\in L^{1+\gamma}_{\mathrm{loc}} \qquad\text{for some }\gamma>0.$$ For every pseudoeffective $\mathbb Q$-divisor $W$ on $Y$ there is an effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor $C_A$ supported on $\operatorname{Supp}A$ such that $W+C_A$ is $\mathbb Q$-linearly equivalent to an effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor.
  - Explanation: Local strictness separates growing jets and proves bigness. A nef and big decomposition with strict discrepancy control then makes any pseudoeffective rational divisor effective up to a correction supported on the given boundary.
- **claim-supported-fano-type-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-fano-model)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective. Suppose $C\geq0$ is a rational divisor with $\kappa(Y,C)=0$, and $\Delta\geq0$ is big and rational with $(Y,\Delta)$ klt and $K_Y+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}C$. Then there is a birational contraction $\chi:Y\dashrightarrow Y_0$ to a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial variety of Fano type. Every prime contracted by $\chi$ lies in $\operatorname{Supp}C$. Every pseudoeffective rational divisor on $Y_0$ is $\mathbb Q$-linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor.
  - Explanation: An effective big klt boundary whose adjoint is rationally equivalent to an effective divisor of vanishing Iitaka dimension admits a Fano-type auxiliary model. Contracted primes remain inside the prescribed support.
- **claim-invariant-index-to-forms** (construction, [source](#stmt-inv-forms)): Let $X$ be a compact Kähler manifold, let a compact real torus $T_c$ act holomorphically, and give $L=-K_X$ its natural linearization. Suppose $L$ has a smooth semipositive metric and $$I_{T_c}(0):=\sum_q(-1)^q\dim H^q(X,\mathcal O_X)^{T_c}\ne0.$$ There are a fixed $p\ge0$ and unbounded positive integers $m$ with $H^0(X,\Omega_X^p\otimes(m+1)L)^{T_c}\ne0$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero invariant index at the actual zero value gives invariant cohomology at unbounded positive exponents. Equivariant hard Lefschetz produces forms with the required coefficient shift.
- **claim-natural-invariant-anticanonical-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inv-nonvanishing)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective with $\chi(X,\mathcal O_X)\ne0$. Let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$, and suppose $L=-K_X$ is smoothly semipositive. For the natural anticanonical linearization, $H^0(X,mL)^T\ne0$ for some positive integer $m$.
  - Explanation: For a smooth connected projective variety with nonzero holomorphic Euler characteristic and smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle, an algebraic torus admits a nonzero anticanonical section for the natural linearization.
- **claim-invariant-determinant-null-slope** (bound, [source](#stmt-inv-null-slope)): If $\nu<n$, the determinant line just constructed satisfies $$c_1(A)\cdot[\theta]^\nu[\omega_0]^{n-1-\nu}\le0.$$
  - Explanation: Below full curvature rank, the determinant line obtained from invariant forms has nonpositive intersection against the curvature-null test class.
- **claim-isometric-liouville-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inv-liouville-recovery)): Let $d>0$ be an integer. Suppose a compact complex manifold $X'$ is the quotient of $\mathbb C^a\times W\times F$ by a group acting by product maps, whose translation projection is a full lattice in $\mathbb C^a$. Assume $W,F$ are connected compact complex manifolds, the canonical bundles of $\mathbb C^a$ and $W$ have invariant nowhere-vanishing frames, and monodromy on $H^0(F,-dK_F)$ preserves a Hermitian norm. A nonzero section of $-dK_{X'}$ determines a nonzero monodromy-invariant section of $-dK_F$.
  - Explanation: Invariant canonical frames remove the first factors from the lifted section. Norm-preserving monodromy makes the remaining holomorphic family bounded, so it is constant and gives an invariant section on the compact factor.
- **claim-extremal-character-invariant-tensors** (construction, [source](#stmt-ext-invariant-tensors)): Let $F$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety such that $H^0(F,\Omega_F^i)=0$ for every $i>0$. Suppose $L_F=-K_F$ has a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Let an algebraic torus $T$ act effectively on $F$, and give $L_F$ its natural tangent-determinant linearization. Then there are an integer $p\geq0$ and unbounded positive integers $k$ such that $$H^0\bigl(F,(\Omega_F^1)^{\otimes p}\otimes kL_F\bigr)^T\ne0.$$ The trivial torus and the point variety are included.
  - Explanation: Vanishing of all positive-degree holomorphic forms, smooth anticanonical semipositivity, and an effective naturally linearized torus action yield invariant cotangent tensors of fixed degree at unbounded positive twists. Extremal source characters are canceled before ordinary conversion.

For a sufficiently large divisible integer $r$, resolve the complete system $|r(-K_Y+J-A)|$ and $J$ by $\\sigma:Y'\\to Y$, and write $$\\sigma^*|r(-K_Y+J-A)|=G+|\\mathrm{Mov}|.$$ Then $\\Psi=G/r-\\sigma^*J-K_{Y'/Y}$ has coefficients less than one, $\\Psi^+$ is an effective klt boundary, and $D_0=\\Psi^-$ is effective and supported on $\\sigma^*J$ and exceptional primes. Moreover $$\\label{eq:weighted-adjoint-identity}\n D_0\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}\n K_{Y'}+\\Psi^++\\mathrm{Mov}/r+\\sigma^*A.$$ The rational class $\\mathrm{Mov}/r+\\sigma^*A$ is nef and big. If an algebraic torus acts on $Y$, $J$ is invariant, and the indicated full systems are torus-stable, the resolution may be chosen equivariantly. Then $G$, $K_{Y'/Y}$, $\\Psi^\\pm$ and $D_0$ are invariant, and the free system $|\\mathrm{Mov}|$ is torus-stable. Identity <a href=\"#eq:weighted-adjoint-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-adjoint-identity\">[eq:weighted-adjoint-identity]</a> is an identity of ordinary rational line bundles; it does not assert that a chosen member of either the free system or the ample class is invariant.",
      "explanation": "The signed approximation separates an effective klt boundary from a supported correction and retains a nef and big moving class. Equivariance controls the systems and divisors, while the adjoint identity remains an ordinary line-bundle identity.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-weighted-approximation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be smooth projective and let $J\\ge0$ be integral. For a sufficiently large divisible integer $r$, resolve the complete system $|r(-K_Y+J-A)|$ and $J$ by $\\sigma:Y'\\to Y$, and write $$\\sigma^*|r(-K_Y+J-A)|=G+|\\mathrm{Mov}|.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-068.6"
        },

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-weighted-approximation",
          "exact_quote": "If an algebraic torus acts on $Y$, $J$ is invariant, and the indicated full systems are torus-stable, the resolution may be chosen equivariantly.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.6"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-correction-ring",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be smooth projective of positive dimension, and let $A_0$ be a rational divisor. Suppose $-K_Y+A_0$ has a semipositive singular metric with locally integrable squared frame norms, smooth and strictly positive on a nonempty ordinary open set.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-conv-return",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:Z\\to X$ be a projective birational morphism of smooth projective varieties and let $f:Z\\to Y$ be a surjective morphism to a smooth projective variety with connected general fibers, and let $L$ be a rational line bundle on $X$. Assume every prime of $Z$ mapping into codimension at least two in $Y$ is $\\pi$-exceptional. Let $R\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}a\\pi^*L$, $a>0$, be a rational divisor with nonnegative horizontal part. For each base prime $Q$ put $$\\mu_Q(R)=\\min_{D\\mapsto Q}\n \\frac{\\operatorname{ord}_D R}{\\operatorname{ord}_D f^*Q},\n \\qquad M=\\sum_Q\\mu_Q(R)Q.$$ Suppose $M$ is pseudoeffective. If there is an effective rational $C_0$, all components of whose pullback are $\\pi$-exceptional, such that $M+C_0\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}G\\ge0$, then $L$ is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor. The same conclusion holds if the premise is $\\epsilon M+C_0\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}G\\ge0$ for some rational $\\epsilon>0$.*\n\n*For the invariant conclusion, give $L$ a specified linearization and assume $$R=c\\,\\operatorname{div}(\\pi^*t),\\qquad cr=a,$$ where $c>0$ is rational and $t$ is a nonzero invariant rational section of the specified linearized $L^r$, with $r>0$ an integer for which $L^r$ is a line bundle. Assume also that every function pulled back from $Y$ is invariant in $\\mathbb C(X)$. Then the resulting section of a positive multiple of $L$ is invariant for that linearization; no regular group action on $Z$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.6"
      },

        {

          "anchor": "stmt-conv-return",
          "exact_quote": "If there is an effective rational $C_0$, all components of whose pullback are $\\pi$-exceptional, such that $M+C_0\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}G\\ge0$,",
          "source_id": "PM-068.6"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-conv-return",
          "exact_quote": "Assume also that every function pulled back from $Y$ is invariant in $\\mathbb C(X)$. Then the resulting section of a positive multiple of $L$ is invariant for that linearization; no regular group action on $Z$ is required.",

      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-conv-return",
          "exact_quote": "For each base prime $Q$ put $$\\mu_Q(R)=\\min_{D\\mapsto Q}\n \\frac{\\operatorname{ord}_D R}{\\operatorname{ord}_D f^*Q},\n \\qquad M=\\sum_Q\\mu_Q(R)Q.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-068.6"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-conv-return",
          "exact_quote": "If there is an effective rational $C_0$, all components of whose pullback are $\\pi$-exceptional, such that $M+C_0\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}G\\ge0$, then $L$ is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor.           "source_id": "PM-068.6"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-coa-curvature",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:\\mathcal Z\\to\\mathbb D$ be a proper holomorphic submersion from a Kähler manifold, with fibers of complex dimension $r>0$. Let $(\\mathcal L,h)$ be a holomorphic line bundle with a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature. Suppose $\\mathcal E=f_*(K_{\\mathcal Z/\\mathbb D}+\\mathcal L)$ is locally free and commutes with base change. Give $\\mathcal E$ its fiberwise $L^2$ metric. Write $D'$ for the $(1,0)$ part of the Chern connection of this direct-image metric. For a local holomorphic section $v$ with $D'v(0)=0$, there is a smooth absolute $\\mathcal L$-valued $(r,0)$-form $w$ representing $v$ such that $$\\label{coa:curvature-bound}\n -i\\partial\\bar\\partial\\|v\\|^2\\big|_0\n \\ \\geq\\\n f_*\\bigl(c_r w\\wedge\\overline w\\,h\\wedge i\\Theta_{\\mathcal L,h}\\bigr)\n \\big|_0,\\qquad c_r=i^{r^2}.$$ In particular, if $i\\Theta_{\\mathcal L,h}\\geq c f^*(i\\,dt\\wedge d\\bar t)$ on a nonempty open subset of the central fiber and its neighborhood, where $c>0$ and $v(0)$ does not vanish identically on that subset, then the curvature pairing of $\\mathcal E$ with $v(0)$ is strictly positive at $0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.6"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-coa-volume-metric",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ and $Z$ be connected smooth projective varieties, let $g:Z\\to X$ be a birational morphism, and let $f:Z\\to Y$ be a surjective morphism to a connected smooth projective variety of positive dimension. Put $L=-K_X$, and assume $L$ has a smooth Hermitian metric $h$ with semipositive curvature. Let $E=K_{Z/X}$ be the effective Jacobian divisor. Assume $$\\operatorname{rank} f_*\\mathcal O_Z(E)=1.$$ Let $\\mathcal S$ be the set of prime divisors $G$ on $Y$ such that every prime divisor of $Z$ dominating $G$ is $g$-exceptional. Suppose there is an open patch over the smooth base-change locus of $f$, disjoint from $E$, on which the curvature of $g^*h$ dominates a positive multiple of the pullback of a Kähler form on $Y$. Then there is an effective integral divisor $A$ supported on $\\mathcal S$ and a singular metric on $M=-K_Y+A$, with psh weights $\\psi$, such that the metric is smooth and strictly positively curved on a nonempty open subset of $Y$ and $$\\label{coa:weighted-lp}\n e^{-\\psi}|s_A|^2\\in L^{1+\\eta}_{\\mathrm{loc}}\n \\qquad\\text{for some }\\eta>0.$$ Here $|s_A|$ means the absolute value of the coefficient of the canonical section in a local holomorphic frame.",

Assume $$\\operatorname{rank} f_*\\mathcal O_Z(E)=1.$$ Let $\\mathcal S$ be the set of prime divisors $G$ on $Y$ such that every prime divisor of $Z$ dominating $G$ is $g$-exceptional. Suppose there is an open patch over the smooth base-change locus of $f$, disjoint from $E$, on which the curvature of $g^*h$ dominates a positive multiple of the pullback of a Kähler form on $Y$. Then there is an effective integral divisor $A$ supported on $\\mathcal S$ and a singular metric on $M=-K_Y+A$, with psh weights $\\psi$, such that the metric is smooth and strictly positively curved on a nonempty open subset of $Y$ and $$\\label{coa:weighted-lp}\n e^{-\\psi}|s_A|^2\\in L^{1+\\eta}_{\\mathrm{loc}}\n \\qquad\\text{for some }\\eta>0.$$ Here $|s_A|$ means the absolute value of the coefficient of the canonical section in a local holomorphic frame.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.6"

Suppose there is an open patch over the smooth base-change locus of $f$, disjoint from $E$, on which the curvature of $g^*h$ dominates a positive multiple of the pullback of a Kähler form on $Y$. Then there is an effective integral divisor $A$ supported on $\\mathcal S$ and a singular metric on $M=-K_Y+A$, with psh weights $\\psi$, such that the metric is smooth and strictly positively curved on a nonempty open subset of $Y$ and $$\\label{coa:weighted-lp}\n e^{-\\psi}|s_A|^2\\in L^{1+\\eta}_{\\mathrm{loc}}\n \\qquad\\text{for some }\\eta>0.$$ Here $|s_A|$ means the absolute value of the coefficient of the canonical section in a local holomorphic frame.",
      "explanation": "Ordinary adjoint rank and horizontal strictness give an integral correction supported on exceptional base primes and an extended semipositive metric whose distinguished section has higher integrability.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ and $Z$ be connected smooth projective varieties, let $g:Z\\to X$ be a birational morphism, and let $f:Z\\to Y$ be a surjective morphism to a connected smooth projective variety of positive dimension. Assume $$\\operatorname{rank} f_*\\mathcal O_Z(E)=1.$$ Let $\\mathcal S$ be the set of prime divisors $G$ on $Y$ such that every prime divisor of $Z$ dominating $G$ is $g$-exceptional. Suppose there is an open patch over the smooth base-change locus of $f$, disjoint from $E$, on which the curvature of $g^*h$ dominates a positive multiple of the pullback of a Kähler form on $Y$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-coa-weighted-effectivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be smooth connected projective of positive dimension, let $A\\geq0$ be integral, and put $M=-K_Y+A$.         "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective variety of dimension $b>0$, let $A\\geq0$ be an integral divisor, and put $M=-K_Y+A$.         "anchor": "stmt-fano-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be smooth projective. Suppose $C\\geq0$ is a rational divisor with $\\kappa(Y,C)=0$, and $\\Delta\\geq0$ is big and rational with $(Y,\\Delta)$ klt and $K_Y+\\Delta\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}C$.         "exact_quote": "Let $d>0$ be an integer. Suppose a compact complex manifold $X'$ is the quotient of $\\mathbb C^a\\times W\\times F$ by a group acting by product maps, whose translation projection is a full lattice in $\\mathbb C^a$.         "source_id": "PM-068.6"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ext-invariant-tensors",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Multiplier ideal of a singular metric** ([source](#pm-h-2)): If the squared norm in a local frame of a singular metric $h$ is $e^{-\phi}$, its multiplier ideal $\mathcal J(h)$ consists of the holomorphic germs $u$ with $\|u\|^2e^{-\phi}$ locally integrable.
- **Distinguished boundary-section integrability** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Thus $\mathcal J(h)\supset\mathcal O_Y(-C)$ means precisely that the canonical section $s_C$ of the effective integral divisor $C$ has locally finite squared norm.
- **Rationally effective divisor** ([source](#pm-h-2)): We call a rational divisor *rationally effective* if it is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor.
- **Pseudoeffective rational divisor** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A rational divisor is *pseudoeffective* when its numerical class lies in the closure of the cone generated by effective divisors.
- **Klt effective rational boundary** ([source](#pm-h-3)): An effective rational boundary $\Delta$ is *klt* (Kawamata log terminal) if, on a log resolution $\sigma:Y'\to Y$, every coefficient of $\sigma^*(K_Y+\Delta)-K_{Y'}$ is less than one.
- **Global strict curvature lower bound** ([source](#sec:approximation)): A global strict lower bound means $\sqrt{-1}\partial\bar\partial\phi\ge c\omega$ for one Kähler form $\omega$ and one $c>0$.
- **Relative canonical Jacobian divisor** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Write $K_{Z/X}=K_Z-\pi^*K_X$ for the relative canonical (Jacobian) divisor.
- **Admissible subsystem** ([source](#conv:all-polarization)): Call a subsystem of an integral divisor $D$ admissible if it has a nonzero section and $bL-D$ is pseudoeffective for some integer $b>0$.
- **Rational map dominated by the anticanonical bundle** ([source](#coa:maximal-base)): A rational map is *dominated by $L$* if, on a smooth resolution $X\xleftarrow{\ g\ }Z\xrightarrow{\ f\ }Y$, one has $$g^*L-\epsilon f^*H\quad\hbox{pseudoeffective}$$ for an ample $H$ on the projective base and a rational $\epsilon>0$.
- **Natural anticanonical linearization** ([source](#inv:section)): Here the natural linearization on $-K_X=\det T_X$ is the action induced by the differential of the action on $X$.
- **Simplicial toroidal condition** ([source](#inv:toroidal-volume)): For clarity, the simplicial condition means that the primitive rays of each local toric cone are linearly independent.
- **Left action on sections** ([source](#ext:section)): $$ The trivial torus and the point variety are included.* </div> We use the left action on sections: a group element transports a fiber value and changes its argument by the inverse action.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-weighted-logarithmic-threshold | a | < | $j+1$ | The exponent and section vanishing order satisfy the nonnegativity conditions in the source. |
| q-unweighted-logarithmic-threshold | a | < | $1$ | The logarithmic exponent is nonnegative. |
| q-resolution-strict-discrepancy | \frac ar-j-k | < | $1$ | The fixed-divisor, boundary, and Jacobian orders are those defined in the proof. |
| q-single-iitaka-adjoint-rank | \operatorname{rank}f_*\mathcal O_Z(E) | = | $1$ | The relative canonical divisor and base polarization are fixed by the construction. |
| q-primitive-curvature-normalization | c_r | = | $i^{r^2}$ | The positive fiber dimension determines the differential-form normalization. |
| q-coarea-integrability-margin | \eta | > | $0$ | The integrability margin depends on the morphism and its Jacobian ideal; the statement supplies no numerical choice. |
| q-integral-boundary-ramification-correction | a_G | ≥ | $\max\{0,(h_J+1)/e_J-1\}$ | The Jacobian order and positive ramification multiplicity determine an allowed integral correction coefficient. |
| q-weighted-metric-mixing-condition | \frac{1-\sigma}{1+\eta}+\frac{\sigma}{\tau} | < | $1$ | The positive integrability margins determine how small the mixing fraction must be. |
| q-jet-section-growth | h^0(Y,hM+B_1) | ≥ | $c' h^b$ | The dimension and local curvature data determine an existential positive growth constant and a sufficiently large exponent regime. |
| q-jet-strict-discrepancy | \frac r{mk} | < | $1+a+c$ | The multiplier-ideal, Jacobian, and boundary orders are fixed by the resolution. |
| q-invariant-null-intersection | c_1(A)\cdot[\theta]^\nu[\omega_0]^{n-1-\nu} | ≤ | $0$ | The maximum curvature rank is strictly below the variety dimension. |
| q-source-euler-characteristic | \chi(P_s,\mathcal O_{P_s}) | = | $1$ | Positive-degree holomorphic-form vanishing descends to the source through the dominant rational limit map. |
| q-source-character-cancellation | \sum_s b_sw_{P_s} | = | $0$ | Nonnegative integral multiplicities are not all zero. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-weighted-resolution** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-resolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-resolution): Combine Nadel vanishing and regularity with point extension, then use transverse integrability to obtain strict signed discrepancies. -> Strict discrepancy bounds and controlled support of the negative part.
- **step-weighted-approximation** [claim](#stmt-thm-weighted-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-weighted-approximation): Subtract a small ample metric and separate the signed divisor into its positive and negative parts. -> An adjoint identity with an effective klt positive part and a supported correction.
- **step-rational-recovery** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-recovery): Express effectivity as a finite rational polyhedron and recover a rational feasible point. -> An effective rational-linear representative.
- **step-controlled-boundary** [claim](#stmt-thm-controlled-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-recovery-2): Insert a small pseudoeffective perturbation into the ample reserve, construct a big klt boundary, apply nonvanishing, and push the correction down. -> Rational effectivity with correction supported on the prescribed boundary.
- **step-fixed-correction** [claim](#stmt-prop-ample-perturbation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ample-perturbation): Retain the approximation data before choosing the pseudoeffective direction. -> A fixed supported divisor with direction-dependent positive scaling.
- **step-pseudoeffective-metric-perturbation** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-pseudoeffective-perturbation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-pseudoeffective-perturbation): Use strong openness, small-exponent integrability, and Hölder on a finite coordinate covering. -> A small perturbed metric retaining strictness and distinguished-section integrability.
- **step-integrable-neighborhood** [claim](#stmt-lem-integrable-neighborhood) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-integrable-neighborhood): Deduce bigness from positive curvature mass, mix metrics to obtain a global strict bound, and approximate simultaneous perturbed directions. -> Effective klt representatives after subtracting a common ample reserve.
- **step-big-klt-representatives** [claim](#stmt-cor-big-klt-representative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-big-klt-representative): Add a general small-coefficient representative of the ample reserve and check discrepancies on a common resolution. -> Effective big klt representatives.
- **step-correction-ring** [claim](#stmt-thm-correction-ring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-correction-ring): Apply multigraded adjoint finite generation, clear denominators and rational equivalences, and pass to a finite-index Veronese. -> Finite generation for the actual Cartier correction divisors.
- **step-return-sections** [claim](#stmt-conv-return) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-return): Use vertical minima to bound coefficients, discard only birationally exceptional negative primes, and clear denominators after pushforward. -> A section on the original variety, invariant when the additional invariant premises hold.
- **step-single-iitaka-conversion** [claim](#stmt-conv-fixed-error) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-return-2): Prepare one Iitaka fibration, derive affine divisor identities and pseudoeffective vertical minima, establish ordinary adjoint rank, and construct a strict base metric by singular direct-image positivity. -> An untwisted positive anticanonical section.
- **step-null-curvature** [claim](#stmt-conv-null-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-null-curvature): Compare mixed nef intersections using the pseudoeffective difference, deduce pointwise wedge vanishing, and bound eigenvalues on a constant-rank patch. -> Kernel inclusion and local horizontal curvature domination.
- **step-unweighted-effectivity** [claim](#stmt-conv-unweighted-effectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-unweighted-effectivity): Construct a perturbed big klt representative, apply big-boundary nonvanishing, and recover rational equivalence. -> Effectivity of the correction plus a small multiple of the direction.
- **step-determinant-extraction** [claim](#stmt-conv-determinant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-determinant): Saturate the generic span, use cotangent-subsheaf pseudoeffectivity, and wedge a varying section with a fixed generic basis. -> Effective twists with a fixed pseudoeffective Cartier error and preserved invariant determinants.
- **step-tensor-conversion** [claim](#stmt-conv-tensor-conversion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-tensor-conversion): Apply determinant extraction and fixed-error conversion; include exterior forms through alternation. -> An ordinary anticanonical section.
- **step-primitive-curvature** [claim](#stmt-coa-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coa-curvature): Solve simultaneously for a primitive absolute representative using the coefficient Green operator and differentiate the fiber norm. -> A direct-image curvature lower bound and strictness detected on a horizontal patch.
- **step-coarea-integrability** [claim](#stmt-coa-coarea) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coa-coarea): Resolve the Jacobian-minor ideal, integrate small inverse powers, and combine coarea with the power-mean inequality. -> Absolute continuity and higher integrability of the pushforward density.
- **step-smooth-volume-metric** [claim](#stmt-coa-volume-metric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coa-volume-metric): Identify the Jacobian section's fiber norm with the volume density, correct ramification exponents, and extend the weights across the boundary. -> A supported integral correction and a metric with weighted higher integrability.
- **step-invariant-volume-metric** [claim](#stmt-coa-invariant-volume-metric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coa-invariant-volume-metric): Average onto the invariant line as a holomorphic orthogonal projection and retain the same ramification and coarea estimates. -> The supported smooth volume-metric conclusion with invariant rank in place of ordinary rank.
- **step-weighted-globalization** [claim](#stmt-coa-weighted-effectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coa-weighted-effectivity): Use positive curvature mass to obtain bigness, then mix with a strict divisorial metric within the higher-integrability margin. -> A globally strict weighted metric and supported effectivity.
- **step-jet-base-effectivity** [claim](#stmt-jet-base-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-base-lemma): Separate growing jets by Nadel vanishing, prove bigness, resolve a globally generated multiplier-ideal system, and perturb the resulting nef and big class while preserving strict discrepancies. -> Rational effectivity with a correction confined to the original boundary.
- **step-supported-fano-model** [claim](#stmt-fano-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fano-model): Take a log terminal model, use the negativity identity to locate contracted primes, trivialize the semiample adjoint, and perturb the big boundary to obtain Fano type. -> An auxiliary Fano-type model with supported contractions and rational effectivity of pseudoeffective divisors.
- **step-invariant-forms** [claim](#stmt-inv-forms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inv-forms): Use invariant-index polynomiality at the actual zero value and compact-averaged hard Lefschetz with the canonical coefficient shift. -> Invariant twisted forms of a fixed degree at unbounded positive exponents.
- **step-invariant-null-slope** [claim](#stmt-inv-null-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inv-null-slope): Apply prescribed-Ricci metrics and the integrated Bochner formula, then inspect the leading intersection coefficient. -> A nonpositive determinant intersection.
- **step-invariant-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-inv-nonvanishing) / [proof](#inv:toroidal-volume): Construct the invariant ratio field, establish invariant adjoint rank and horizontal strictness, verify all-valuation integrability on a simplicial toroidal model, and return an invariant rational section. -> A naturally invariant anticanonical section.
- **step-liouville-recovery** [claim](#stmt-inv-liouville-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inv-liouville-recovery): Trivialize invariant canonical frames, remove dependence on the compact factor, and apply boundedness and Liouville to the translation-factor family. -> A nonzero monodromy-invariant section on the remaining compact factor.
- **step-extremal-character-tensors** [claim](#stmt-ext-invariant-tensors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-invariant-tensors): Identify maximal source characters by orbit degrees, isolate their localization coefficients, cancel a nonnegative integral combination of characters, and lift cohomology characters by equivariant hard Lefschetz. -> Invariant twisted cotangent tensors of fixed degree at unbounded positive exponents.

          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be smooth projective and let $J\\ge0$ be integral. Suppose $-K_Y+J$ has a singular metric with a global strict curvature lower bound and multiplier ideal containing $\\mathcal O_Y(-J)$. Choose a sufficiently small ample rational divisor $A$. For a sufficiently large divisible integer $r$, resolve the complete system $|r(-K_Y+J-A)|$ and $J$ by $\\sigma:Y'\\to Y$, and write $$\\sigma^*|r(-K_Y+J-A)|=G+|\\mathrm{Mov}|.$$ Then $\\Psi=G/r-\\sigma^*J-K_{Y'/Y}$ has coefficients less than one, $\\Psi^+$ is an effective klt boundary, and $D_0=\\Psi^-$ is effective and supported on $\\sigma^*J$ and exceptional primes. Moreover $$\\label{eq:weighted-adjoint-identity}\n D_0\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}\n K_{Y'}+\\Psi^++\\mathrm{Mov}/r+\\sigma^*A.$$ The rational class $\\mathrm{Mov}/r+\\sigma^*A$ is nef and big. If an algebraic torus acts on $Y$, $J$ is invariant, and the indicated full systems are torus-stable, the resolution may be chosen equivariantly. Then $G$, $K_{Y'/Y}$, $\\Psi^\\pm$ and $D_0$ are invariant, and the free system $|\\mathrm{Mov}|$ is torus-stable. Identity <a href=\"#eq:weighted-adjoint-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-adjoint-identity\">[eq:weighted-adjoint-identity]</a> is an identity of ordinary rational line bundles; it does not assert that a chosen member of either the free system or the ample class is invariant.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.6"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-weighted-approximation",
      "inputs": [
        "Weighted resolution",
        "A small ample reserve"
      ],
      "method": "Subtract a small ample metric and separate the signed divisor into its positive and negative parts.",
      "output": "An adjoint identity with an effective klt positive part and a supported correction.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-weighted-approximation"
    },

The same conclusion holds if the premise is $\\epsilon M+C_0\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}G\\ge0$ for some rational $\\epsilon>0$.*\n\n*For the invariant conclusion, give $L$ a specified linearization and assume $$R=c\\,\\operatorname{div}(\\pi^*t),\\qquad cr=a,$$ where $c>0$ is rational and $t$ is a nonzero invariant rational section of the specified linearized $L^r$, with $r>0$ an integer for which $L^r$ is a line bundle. Assume also that every function pulled back from $Y$ is invariant in $\\mathbb C(X)$. Then the resulting section of a positive multiple of $L$ is invariant for that linearization; no regular group action on $Z$ is required.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-controlled-correction-support**: Every pseudoeffective rational divisor becomes rationally effective after adding an effective rational correction supported on the prescribed integral boundary.
- **invariant-actual-divisor-ring**: Nearby rational directions admit common Cartier multiples whose multigraded section ring is finitely generated.
- **invariant-fixed-error-conversion**: An unbounded sequence of effective anticanonical twists with a fixed pseudoeffective Cartier error yields an untwisted positive anticanonical multiple with a section.
- **invariant-natural-linearization**: The invariant anticanonical nonvanishing conclusion uses the natural anticanonical linearization.
- **invariant-extremal-character-tensors**: Vanishing of positive-degree holomorphic forms and smooth anticanonical semipositivity permit invariant twisted cotangent tensors of fixed tensor degree at unbounded positive line exponents.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-weighted-as-unweighted**: Apply nearby-ring finite generation when only the distinguished boundary section has locally finite norm. -> Vanishing of the boundary section can compensate for a singular frame norm, leaving the unweighted integrability premise unproved.
- **anti-use-discard-boundary-correction**: Conclude rational effectivity of the original pseudoeffective divisor without retaining the correction. -> The guaranteed effective class includes a boundary-supported addition.
- **anti-use-finite-sequence-conversion**: Infer anticanonical nonvanishing from a finite collection of twisted sections. -> The proof obtains nonnegative horizontal slopes and pseudoeffective vertical minima by passing along unbounded exponents.
- **anti-use-ordinary-as-invariant**: Treat ordinary tensor conversion as a guarantee of an invariant scalar section. -> Ordinary conversion does not control the character of the resulting section.
- **anti-use-auxiliary-fano-as-source-fano**: Declare the original variety to be of Fano type because the auxiliary base admits a Fano-type model. -> The contraction theorem applies to the auxiliary base; returning a section to the original variety supplies a different conclusion.
- **anti-use-drop-form-vanishing**: Apply the extremal-character tensor result to a compact factor without checking vanishing of positive-degree holomorphic forms. -> The source Euler characteristic argument depends on that vanishing and supplies the nonzero coefficient used in localization.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal results assert divisor effectivity, finite generation, and section existence on smooth projective varieties through analytic metric constructions and birational nonvanishing. The controlled-boundary proof explicitly invokes big-boundary nonvanishing to obtain an effective real divisor before rational recovery. The fixed-error theorem requires an unbounded sequence, and the ring theorem asserts finite generation without supplying generators or an effective stopping bound. Rational recovery uses a finite rational polyhedron after supports and rational functions have been supplied, but checking such auxiliary coefficient data would not decide the analytic hypotheses or certify the principal geometric conclusions. A finite executable construction with an independent oracle for these conclusions is ABSENT.

The fixed-error theorem requires an unbounded sequence, and the ring theorem asserts finite generation without supplying generators or an effective stopping bound. Rational recovery uses a finite rational polyhedron after supports and rational functions have been supplied, but checking such auxiliary coefficient data would not decide the analytic hypotheses or certify the principal geometric conclusions. A finite executable construction with an independent oracle for these conclusions is ABSENT.",
    "reference_code": null,
    "test_vectors": []
  },
  "pm_id": "PM-068.6",
  "schema": "pm.triptych.v2"
}
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-controlled-boundary-analytic-premises** (hypothesis): Controlled-boundary effectivity requires smooth projectivity, positive dimension, an effective integral boundary, global strict curvature domination, and integrability of the distinguished boundary section. Blocked use: ['Applying the criterion from pseudoeffectivity alone.', 'Replacing the prescribed strict metric premise with mere semipositivity.']
- **limit-correction-cannot-be-discarded** (formal_scope): The effectivity conclusion concerns the divisor after adding a correction supported on the boundary. Blocked use: ['Claiming an uncorrected effective representative without an additional argument removing the correction.']
- **limit-weighted-versus-frame-integrability** (hypothesis): Integrability of a vanishing distinguished section does not establish integrability of a nonvanishing frame. Blocked use: ['Invoking the correction-ring theorem using weighted section integrability alone.']
- **limit-resolution-divisibility-and-strictness** (hypothesis): The approximation requires sufficiently large divisible exponents and a resolution of the stated supports; its discrepancy inequalities must remain strict. Blocked use: ['Using an arbitrary small exponent.', 'Treating non-strict discrepancy inequalities as sufficient for a klt boundary.']
- **limit-equivariant-systems-not-invariant-members** (formal_scope): Equivariant approximation controls divisors and free systems but does not assert that chosen free or ample representatives are invariant. Blocked use: ['Inferring invariant representatives from stability of the complete system.', 'Reading the adjoint identity as an identity asserting invariant chosen sections.']
- **limit-rational-recovery-supplied-witness** (hypothesis): Rational recovery assumes an effective real-linear representative of a rational divisor. Its finite coefficient argument begins with the principal divisors appearing in that representative. Blocked use: ['Using rational recovery as a decision procedure for arbitrary pseudoeffective divisors.', 'Assuming the initial effective real representative has been constructed.']
- **limit-adjoint-nonvanishing-existential** (nonconstructive): The principal boundary construction obtains an effective real representative from big-boundary nonvanishing before rational recovery; no finite section-finding procedure is supplied. Blocked use: ['Advertising the argument as an executable construction of effective representatives from metric input.']
- **limit-fixed-support-variable-scaling** (formal_scope): The supported divisor can be fixed independently of the pseudoeffective direction, but the scaling and general ample representative depend on that direction. Blocked use: ['Claiming a uniform positive scaling for every pseudoeffective direction.']
- **limit-metric-perturbation-small-pseudoeffective-direction** (hypothesis): The weighted perturbation lemma permits a sufficiently small positive rational perturbation in a pseudoeffective direction while retaining the original controlled-boundary assumptions. Blocked use: ['Applying the perturbation statement to arbitrary directions or arbitrary perturbation sizes.']
- **limit-integrable-neighborhood-finite-directions** (hypothesis): The integrable neighborhood requires unweighted squared frame norms and smooth strict positivity on a nonempty ordinary open set. Simultaneous perturbation is asserted for a finite list of rational directions. Blocked use: ['Extending the common perturbation parameter to an unrestricted family of directions.', 'Omitting unweighted integrability or the strict open patch.']
- **limit-ring-actual-divisors-no-generator-bound** (formal_scope): Finite generation concerns common Cartier multiples of actual rational divisors. The theorem does not enumerate generators or provide a bound on their multidegrees. Blocked use: ['Substituting numerical equivalence for the rational linear equivalences used to identify the rings.', 'Inferring a terminating generator-enumeration procedure.']
- **limit-fixed-error-unbounded-sequence** (hypothesis): Fixed-error conversion requires smooth anticanonical semipositivity, the same pseudoeffective Cartier error, and effective integral twists at unbounded positive exponents. Blocked use: ['Applying conversion to a bounded collection of twists.', 'Allowing an unrelated varying error.', 'Replacing smooth metric semipositivity with nefness alone.']
- **limit-return-model-and-exceptional-support** (hypothesis): Returning a section requires nonnegative horizontal coefficients, pseudoeffective vertical minima, exceptional pullback of the correction, and exceptionality of source primes mapping into higher base codimension. Blocked use: ['Discarding negative coefficients at nonexceptional primes.', 'Removing a supported correction without checking its entire pullback.']
- **limit-total-space-ideal-not-all-fibers** (formal_scope): The singular metric used in the direct-image argument has trivial multiplier ideal on the total space; the proof does not assert a uniform corresponding statement on every fiber. Blocked use: ['Replacing the total-space ideal condition with an unproved fiberwise condition.', 'Claiming uniform integrability on all fibers.']
- **limit-null-curvature-maximum-rank-patch** (formal_scope): Kernel inclusion is asserted on the maximum-rank locus. Horizontal domination additionally requires a submersive positive-base-form patch meeting that locus. Blocked use: ['Extending the local domination bound globally.', 'Applying it across a rank-drop locus without additional control.']
- **limit-determinant-fixed-tensor-degree** (hypothesis): Determinant extraction uses a fixed positive cotangent tensor degree, nef anticanonical bundle, and an unbounded positive sequence of twists. Blocked use: ['Using tensor degrees that vary without a fixed-degree subsequence.', 'Applying extraction without the nef anticanonical hypothesis.']
- **limit-ordinary-conversion-no-character-control** (formal_scope): Ordinary tensor conversion does not guarantee an invariant output from invariant determinant inputs. Blocked use: ['Claiming a naturally invariant scalar section solely from ordinary conversion.']
- **limit-curvature-base-change-and-nonzero-section** (hypothesis): The primitive curvature calculation requires a proper Kähler submersion, smooth semipositive coefficients, local freeness, and base change. Strictness additionally requires the section to be nonzero on the horizontal strictness patch. Blocked use: ['Applying the smooth curvature calculation to an arbitrary singular family.', 'Inferring strict direct-image curvature from a patch on which the section vanishes identically.']
- **limit-coarea-exponent-existential** (nonconstructive): The coarea lemma supplies a positive higher-integrability margin for smooth nonnegative volume pushforward under a surjective projective morphism, without an explicit numerical choice. Blocked use: ['Treating the existential margin as a supplied numerical error or stability bound.', 'Extending the result to arbitrary singular measures.']
- **limit-volume-rank-and-horizontal-strictness** (hypothesis): The smooth volume-metric construction requires ordinary adjoint rank and a horizontal strictness patch away from the Jacobian divisor. Its integral correction is supported on primes whose dominating source primes are exceptional. Blocked use: ['Deriving the full extension and integrability conclusion from generic rank alone.', 'Allowing correction support on arbitrary base primes.']
- **limit-invariant-rank-preserved-data** (hypothesis): Replacing ordinary adjoint rank with invariant rank requires a compact torus preserving the maps and smooth metric, acting trivially on the base, with natural canonical linearizations. Blocked use: ['Treating any invariant subbundle of a singular direct image as positive.', 'Dropping preservation of the metric or the trivial base action.']
- **limit-local-to-global-higher-integrability** (hypothesis): The weighted globalization and jet arguments require a smooth strict open patch and a higher-integrability margin for the distinguished section before obtaining a global strict bound. Blocked use: ['Replacing the higher-integrability premise with mere weighted local integrability while retaining these particular globalization conclusions.']
- **limit-fano-model-adjoint-and-support** (hypothesis): The supported Fano-type model requires an effective correction of vanishing Iitaka dimension and an effective big rational klt boundary whose adjoint is rationally equivalent to that correction. Blocked use: ['Applying the contraction theorem without the vanishing Iitaka-dimension premise.', 'Dropping bigness or klt singularities of the boundary.']
- **limit-fano-type-only-auxiliary-model** (formal_scope): The Fano-type conclusion belongs to the contracted auxiliary base, while the return to the original variety establishes an anticanonical section. Blocked use: ['Declaring the original variety to be of Fano type.']
- **limit-invariant-index-actual-zero-and-shift** (hypothesis): The invariant-form construction needs the actual nonzero invariant index at zero, natural linearization, and smooth semipositive coefficients. The canonical coefficient shift must be retained. Blocked use: ['Using polynomiality without control of its actual value at zero.', 'Removing the coefficient shift in the equivariant hard Lefschetz identification.']
- **limit-invariant-nonvanishing-euler-and-linearization** (hypothesis): Invariant anticanonical nonvanishing assumes smooth connected projectivity, nonzero holomorphic Euler characteristic, smooth anticanonical semipositivity, and the natural linearization for an algebraic torus action. Blocked use: ['Transferring the invariant conclusion to an arbitrary character twist of the linearization.', 'Omitting the Euler-characteristic premise.']
- **limit-null-slope-constructed-determinant** (formal_scope): The null-slope bound concerns the determinant line constructed from the invariant forms and applies below full curvature rank. Blocked use: ['Applying the intersection inequality to an arbitrary line bundle.', 'Using the stated null-slope formula outside its rank regime.']
- **limit-signed-boundaries-require-all-twists** (formal_scope): The alternative invariant construction proves pseudoeffectivity by taking the limit of signed boundaries for all positive adjoint twists; a metric for a single twist does not supply that argument. Blocked use: ['Inferring pseudoeffectivity of the limiting slope divisor from only one adjoint metric.']
- **limit-holonomy-vanishing-nontrivial-determinant** (formal_scope): The covariant-tensor vanishing argument uses the nontrivial determinant-character branch of the holonomy discussion; the trivial branch instead gives a Ricci-flat factor with a parallel canonical frame. Blocked use: ['Claiming positive covariant-tensor vanishing for every simply connected Ricci-flat factor.']
- **limit-liouville-isometric-monodromy** (hypothesis): Invariant factor-section recovery requires product deck maps, a full translation lattice, connected compact factors, invariant canonical frames, and monodromy preserving a Hermitian norm. Blocked use: ['Applying the bounded-family argument to non-isometric monodromy.', 'Assuming periodic norm boundedness without a full translation lattice.']
- **limit-extremal-tensors-form-vanishing** (hypothesis): The extremal-character construction requires vanishing of every positive-degree holomorphic form, smooth anticanonical semipositivity, an effective algebraic torus action, and its natural tangent-determinant linearization. Blocked use: ['Dropping form vanishing from the source Euler-characteristic argument.', 'Replacing the natural character convention with an arbitrary linearization.']
- **limit-descent-before-ordinary-conversion** (formal_scope): The tensor descent method retains invariance while transporting tensors to the actual projective finite cover, then applies ordinary conversion and the finite étale norm. Blocked use: ['Inferring invariant scalar descent after ordinary conversion has discarded character control.', 'Using the section norm without the finite étale pullback identification.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>Y</i> be smooth projective and let <i>C</i> be an effective integral divisor. If $`-K_Y+C`$ admits a metric with a global strict curvature lower bound for which the canonical section of <i>C</i> is locally square integrable, every pseudoeffective rational divisor becomes rationally effective after an effective correction supported on <i>C</i>. For a smooth projective <i>X</i> with smoothly semipositive $`L=-K_X`$, sections of $`m_jL-P`$ at unbounded positive exponents, with any fixed pseudoeffective Cartier error <i>P</i>, yield a section of a positive multiple of <i>L</i>.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

A pseudoeffective divisor need not have a section in any positive multiple. This paper studies an analytic condition that makes such a divisor effective up to rational linear equivalence, after a correction on a prescribed boundary. The condition is integrability of the canonical divisor section for a positively curved anticanonical metric. Its usefulness is birational: a correction on the base of a fibration can disappear after pullback and pushforward to the original variety.

If the squared norm in a local frame of a singular metric $h$ is $e^{-\phi}$, its multiplier ideal $\mathcal J(h)$ consists of the holomorphic germs $u$ with $|u|^2e^{-\phi}$ locally integrable. Thus $\mathcal J(h)\supset\mathcal O_Y(-C)$ means precisely that the canonical section $s_C$ of the effective integral divisor $C$ has locally finite squared norm. We call a rational divisor *rationally effective* if it is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor. A rational divisor is *pseudoeffective* when its numerical class lies in the closure of the cone generated by effective divisors. On a smooth projective variety this is equivalent to the existence of a singular Hermitian metric with semipositive curvature current on its rational line bundle.

<a id="stmt-thm-controlled-boundary"></a>
<div id="thm:controlled-boundary" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Effectivity modulo a controlled boundary). *Let $Y$ be a smooth projective complex variety of positive dimension and let $C\ge0$ be an integral divisor. Suppose $-K_Y+C$ has a singular Hermitian metric $h$ whose curvature dominates a Kähler form and for which $$\mathcal J(h)\supset\mathcal O_Y(-C).$$ For every pseudoeffective rational divisor $M$ on $Y$, there is an effective rational divisor $C_0$ supported on $C$ such that $M+C_0$ is rationally effective.*

</div>

The support conclusion distinguishes the theorem from numerical nonvanishing. It permits the correction to be removed under a specified birational map. The analytic assumption also distinguishes two notions of integrability: $s_C$ may have finite norm even when a nonvanishing local frame does not. The stronger, unweighted condition will yield a separate finite-generation result.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The boundary construction and its antecedents

An effective rational boundary $\Delta$ is *klt* (Kawamata log terminal) if, on a log resolution $\sigma:Y'\to Y$, every coefficient of $\sigma^*(K_Y+\Delta)-K_{Y'}$ is less than one. For a simple normal crossing boundary on a smooth variety, this amounts to each boundary coefficient being less than one.

The proof connects multiplier-ideal approximation with big-boundary nonvanishing. Nadel vanishing (Nadel 1990) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Nadel1990](#ref-Nadel1990)], followed by very ample regularity, makes the relevant multiplier-ideal systems globally generated. Point extension of Ohsawa–Takegoshi (Ohsawa and Takegoshi 1987) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[OhsawaTakegoshi1987](#ref-OhsawaTakegoshi1987)], in the weighted Bergman approximation developed by Demailly (Demailly 2015) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Demailly2015](#ref-Demailly2015)], compares the fixed divisor of a complete system with the metric’s singularities. On a resolution $\sigma:Y'\to Y$, weighted integrability produces a signed divisor $\Psi=\Psi^+-\Psi^-$ whose coefficients are strictly below one. Its negative part lies only over $C$ and the exceptional locus.

The approximation leaves an ample rational class $A$ in reserve. For a prescribed pseudoeffective $M$, choose a small rational $t>0$ so that $A+tM$ remains ample. General free and ample representatives then turn the positive part of the signed divisor into an *effective big klt* boundary $\Delta$, with $$K_{Y'}+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}t\sigma^*M+\Psi^-.$$ This is the pair to which the nonvanishing theorem of Birkar–Cascini–Hacon–McKernan (Birkar et al. 2010, Theorem D) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BCHM2010](#ref-BCHM2010)] applies. A finite rational linear system upgrades its real-linear conclusion to rational linear equivalence. Pushforward gives the correction $\sigma_*\Psi^-/t$, supported on $C$. Sections <a href="#sec:approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:approximation">2</a> and <a href="#sec:effectivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:effectivity">3</a> give this construction. The latter also retains an analytic perturbation argument: strong openness of Guan–Zhou (Guan and Zhou 2015) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GuanZhou2015](#ref-GuanZhou2015)] and small-exponent integrability of Skoda (Skoda 1972) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Skoda1972](#ref-Skoda1972)] permit a small pseudoeffective perturbation of the metric while preserving the integrability of the distinguished section.

A different consequence begins with an unweighted integrable semipositive metric that is smooth and strictly positive on one ordinary open set. Boucksom’s volume criterion (Boucksom 2002) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Boucksom2002](#ref-Boucksom2002)] first gives bigness; a small mixture with a strict metric then gives a global curvature lower bound while preserving integrability. Section <a href="#sec:correction-ring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:correction-ring">4</a> makes this preparation explicit and permits perturbation in finitely many arbitrary rational directions. The resulting klt boundaries fit the multigraded adjoint finite-generation results of Cascini–Lazić (Cascini and Lazić 2012) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CasciniLazic2012](#ref-CasciniLazic2012)] and Corti–Lazić (Corti and Lazić 2013, Theorem 3.2(1)) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CortiLazic2013](#ref-CortiLazic2013)]. More precisely, if $-K_Y+A_0$ has this unweighted metric, then for every finite list of rational divisors $P_1,\ldots,P_s$ there are a common rational $\epsilon>0$ and Cartier multiples $B_i=k_0(A_0+\epsilon P_i)$ whose multigraded section ring is finitely generated (Theorem <a href="#thm:correction-ring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:correction-ring">9</a>). This controls the sections of specified Cartier divisors, not merely a numerical cone.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Anticanonical nonvanishing and fixed-error conversion

For a smooth projective variety $X$, anticanonical nonvanishing asks whether some $H^0(X,-mK_X)$, $m>0$, is nonzero when $-K_X$ is nef. Numerical effectivity is weaker: an effective divisor in the numerical class need not supply a section of the actual line bundle. For pairs $(X,\Delta)$, the corresponding divisor is $-(K_X+\Delta)$, the anti-log-canonical divisor. Lazić–Matsumura–Peternell–Tsakanikas–Xie (Lazić et al. 2023, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)] established numerical nonvanishing for projective log canonical threefold pairs with nef anti-log-canonical divisor, assuming either that the variety is $\mathbb Q$-factorial or that it has rational singularities. Müller (Müller 2025, Corollary B) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)] proved nonvanishing for projective klt threefold pairs with nef anti-log-canonical divisor. His higher-dimensional theorem assumes, in addition to nefness, that the anti-log-canonical divisor of the induced pair on the general fiber of the maximal rationally connected fibration is semiample (Müller 2025, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)].

Our principal conversion result uses smooth metric semipositivity and works in every dimension. Let $X$ be smooth connected projective, let $L=-K_X$ carry a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric, and let $P$ be any fixed pseudoeffective Cartier divisor. Then Theorem <a href="#conv:fixed-error" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conv:fixed-error">10</a> gives the implication $$H^0(X,m_jL-P)\ne0\ \text{for unbounded positive }m_j
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 H^0(X,mL)\ne0\ \text{for some }m>0.$$ The fixed error $P$ may itself have no effective representative. A single Iitaka base converts the effective divisors into a pseudoeffective rational divisor on that base. The controlled-boundary theorem makes this divisor rationally effective with correction on base primes whose pullbacks disappear over $X$. A rational function from the base then corrects the original rational section without introducing a pole on $X$.

Twisted differential forms supply the sequence through the determinant method of Lazić–Peternell (Lazić and Peternell 2018, Lemma 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[LP2018](#ref-LP2018)], adapted in (Lazić et al. 2023, Lemma 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)]. Ou’s generic tangent-nefness theorem (Ou 2023, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Ou2023](#ref-Ou2023)], in the cotangent-subsheaf form (Lazić et al. 2023, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)], makes the negative determinant pseudoeffective. Our extraction argument starts with positive unbounded exponents and also includes numerically trivial $L$. Thus nonzero sections of $(\Omega_X^1)^{\otimes p}\otimes m_jL$ for one fixed $p\ge0$ and unbounded positive $m_j$ give an anticanonical section by Corollary <a href="#conv:tensor-conversion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conv:tensor-conversion">15</a>. The same holds for exterior forms. This is an ordinary conversion; invariant sections require further control of characters.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Volume metrics and the distinct geometric refinements

Let $X$ be smooth projective and let $L=-K_X$ carry a smooth semipositive metric. In a resolved rational fibration $$X\xleftarrow{\ \pi\ } Z\xrightarrow{\ g\ }Y,$$ assume $Z,Y$ are smooth projective, $\pi$ is birational, and $g$ is surjective. Write $K_{Z/X}=K_Z-\pi^*K_X$ for the relative canonical (Jacobian) divisor. Its canonical section belongs to $K_Z+\pi^*L$. When $\operatorname{rank}g_*\mathcal O_Z(K_{Z/X})=1$, its squared fiberwise $L^2$ norm is a volume density $\rho$ on the base. On the smooth base-change open where this section generates the adjoint line, $-\log\rho$ is a metric on $-K_Y$. Berndtsson’s direct-image theory supplies its positivity (Berndtsson 2009, 2011) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Berndtsson2009](#ref-Berndtsson2009), [Berndtsson2011](#ref-Berndtsson2011)]. Extending it over the boundary with enough integrability is the geometric task preceding effectivity.

The main conversion proof uses a singular coefficient metric whose curvature dominates a positive base form and whose total-space multiplier ideal is trivial. Singular adjoint direct-image positivity of Păun–Takayama (Păun and Takayama 2018) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[PT2018](#ref-PT2018)] then gives a metric with a global strict lower bound on $-K_Y+C$. Its integral boundary has exceptional pullback, and the distinguished section is locally square integrable.

Keeping the coefficient metric smooth gives a different output. The coarea formula and a primitive-representative curvature calculation produce an integral correction $C$ with $\pi_*g^*C=0$ and $$|s_C|^2e^{-\phi}\in L^{1+\eta}_{\mathrm{loc}}
 \quad\text{for some }\eta>0.$$ The metric is smooth and strictly positive on one ordinary open set. The higher integrability exponent permits a mixture with a globally strict metric while keeping $s_C$ integrable. Thus the global bound needed for Theorem <a href="#thm:controlled-boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:controlled-boundary">1</a> follows from an explicit additional step.
